It is the caller Model's `operation`\n entity and action classes over a machine that keeps the real rows of every reply and adds one row\n the platform never takes: a non-retryable handler error completing the operation as succeeded,\n with the completed event recording it. Its one Query selects that row and its Property names it, so\n the Case the caller realization produces expects a completed event where the platform writes a\n failed one, and every Run of it is violated, deterministically and honestly.\n \n That is what a replay needs before any reducer exists: one Case whose Run is violated and can be\n recorded, admitted, replayed offline and rerun. The control enters no functional, canary or\n exploratory set of the caller Model and no regression view; its Case is registered under\n `temporal.case.nexusCallerControl.<query>` so that `umpire-case` renders its fixture and the live\n suite runs it, and nothing reads it as a claim about the platform. The proposal a replay compiles\n from it proves the promotion mechanism only: its expected trace is the row the platform never\n takes, and it is never reviewed into a regression set.\n \n The forged row is a second result of a row the platform has, not a replaced one: the Case Runtime\n admits a recorded step only as an authorized row, and a rule is violated only when the observed\n step is such a row whose response fails. The real row stays authorized, and since the Producer\n declares the evidence of every result of a witnessed row, the failed event the platform writes is\n lifted and projected to the real row rather than left unread.\n -/\n \n namespace Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Control\n \n open Umpire\n open Umpire.Command\n open Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Caller\n open Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Pair\n \n+-- authoring: derived\n+\n /-! ### The machine\n \n The pair Model's machine -- the operation without its deadlines and retries -- plus the forged row.\n The machine is derived from it rather than copied: every row is the pair machine's, and the one\n function written here returns the one result the platform never takes. -/\n \n /-- The forged row: the non-retryable error completes the operation and records the completed\n event. The `machine` command declares one over a\n   `structure` of finite fields with one step function per action, naming the entity it tracks, the\n   phases it starts and ends in, its timers, its setup parameters and the observation confirming\n   each Fact; `Umpire.Command.MachineDeclaration` is its record.\n+  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* A machine may instead be derived from\n+  another: `from:` names the source, `restrict:` keeps the listed actions' rows and drops the rest,\n+  and `extend:` adds the author's results to an action's rows, in the step order\n+  (`Umpire.Command.Derived`). A derived machine keeps its source's entity, state type, starts, ends\n+  and setup, and the evidence of every fact its rows still record; it reaches its rows through the\n+  source's own step functions, owns its Action catalog and Definition IDs, and inherits neither\n+  `refines:` nor the source's abstract state field.\n - **Entity (`Umpire.Command.Entity`).** Something with identity that a machine keeps state for,\n   declared by the `entity` command with the entities it refers to and the key recorded data names\n   an instance by. A Scenario runs over a number of `instances:` of it. State belongs to machines,\n   not entities: two machines may track one entity differently.\n - **Party.** Who performs an action: a name a Model declares by using it on an `action`. `system`\n   is reserved for the implementation under test, performs no declared action, and owns the\n   timers. A set binds every other party; a fault is an ordinary action of the party that causes\n   it.\n+  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The command that builds one Model from\n+  several machines is `compose`, not `system`: `system` is this reserved party, and a composition is\n+  no party. It declares no action; each of its actions is one or more of its members' actions,\n+  performed by their parties.\n - **Refinement (`Umpire.Command.Refinement`).** A machine that `refines:` another through a state\n   `map:`: a stuttering forward simulation the `machine` command decides over the two tables, so\n   every Property declared on the abstract machine is read on the refining machine's paths. It is\n   not an Implementation Link, which SEM-08 reserves for the connection from `Temporal.Feature` to\n   `Temporal.System`; both machines of a refinement are product behavior.\n+  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* A composition does not refine and\n+  carries no `refines:` or abstract field, though a member may itself be a refining machine; a\n+  derived machine does not inherit its source's refinement.\n+- **Composition (`Umpire.Command.Compose`).** *(drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* One\n+  Model built by the `compose` command from machines of different entities, for a claim no one of\n+  them can state. The author writes the state as a `structure` with one field per member, names each\n+  member machine, and pairs member actions into one step with `sync:` lines; an action no `sync:`\n+  line names steps its member alone. The command generates tagged-union Action, Outcome and Fact\n+  types, keys a composed state by its members' state keys `_`-joined in field order, a member's own\n+  action as `<field>_<key>` and a synchronized action by its `sync:` name, and hangs its Definition\n+  IDs off the owner `compose-<name>`. It elaborates to `Umpire.Command.DeclaredModel` as `machine`\n+  does; no set names one, so a composition answers `verify` Queries only.\n - **Table (`Umpire.FiniteTable`).** The finite row form of a Machine. `Umpire.CheckedTable` is an\n   admitted one and `Umpire.TableModelSpec` is its author record.\n+  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* A composition's table holds only the\n+  rows reachable from its starts, enumerated at elaboration and emitted as a literal; the kernel\n+  decides per composition that the literal agrees with the composition of the members' tables\n+  (`Umpire.Command.ComposeProofs`).\n - **Action.** Something an author asks the Model to do, such as closing a Workflow. Requesting an\n   Action neither chooses its Model Outcome nor proves that the Action occurred at runtime. The\n   `action` command declares one with its party, the entity it creates or acts on, typed inputs\n   over `enum` domains and an optional protobuf schema (`Umpire.Command.Action`). Each member of an\n   input domain is a class, and a constructor with finite fields is one class per assignment of\n   them; a Scenario selects classes, and a machine's step function is written over them.\n - **Model Outcome.** The result the Model produces for an Action. It is an expected model result,\n   not a runtime result.\n - **Step (`Umpire.Step`).** One model step and what it produced: an `outcome`, a `state`, and a list\n   of `facts`.\n - **Fact.** A claim the Model makes at a Step, such as “the Nexus operation received a\n   cancellation.” Logs, spans, RPCs, and records are Evidence used to decide whether the claim held\n   during a Run. Facts are the `facts` of a `Umpire.Step`; the Fact domain is a Model's own type.\n - **Trace (`Umpire.Scenario.Trace`).** A starting state and a sequence of Steps. It contains no\n   runtime Evidence. One position inside a Trace is a `Umpire.ModelCoordinate`.\n - **Scenario (`Umpire.Scenario`).** A named, constrained set of Traces. It defines available\n   variations and faults but selects no single Trace, and it neither evaluates Properties nor\n   determines whether a Trace occurred at runtime. `Umpire.CheckedScenario` is an admitted one.\n - **Property (`Umpire.Property`).** A reusable pass/fail rule over Traces, built from\n   `Umpire.PropertyClause` clauses. For example, closing a Workflow cancels its running Nexus\n   operation at most once. Guarded alternatives inside one clause are branches.\n   `Umpire.CheckedProperty` is an admitted one.\n - **Correlated (`Umpire.Property.Correlated`).** A rule tracked separately per operation and\n   correlated by an explicit key, so one operation's obligations never discharge another's.\n   `Shared.CorrelatedObligation` is the semantics the Lean Producer and the Go runtime share.\n@@ -395,83 +437,100 @@ a declared Nexus history Observation reaches a correlated completion within a bo\n - **AUT-07a — The Model command surface.** *(drafted by fn-83; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)*\n   `Umpire.Command` MAY provide one command surface -- `machine`, `property`, `scenario`, `limits`,\n   `query`, and the `entity`, `enum`, `action` and `observation` declarations they are written over --\n   and it is the only such surface. Module responsibilities\n \n | Module | Responsibility | Normal author |\n | --- | --- | --- |\n | `Umpire` | Domain-neutral semantic authoring, checking, planning, observation, Implementation Link, From the file to a green live test\n+## 13. The worker entity, a second composition, and a derived machine\n+\n+Section 12's composition names the worker entity's module. This section quotes that module, the\n+Outage Model's composition and the derived negative control, and the drift test checks their marked\n+regions as it checks this file's: a region name is unique across every file the walkthrough quotes,\n+and each file closes its last quoted region with its own `-- authoring: end`.\n+\n+**An entity module.** [`Temporal/Feature/Worker/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Worker/Model.lean)\n+declares one entity, the worker of a task queue, and no set, Case or Query: nothing in it is\n+realized on its own. Its machine is what a composition synchronizes with. The handler's worker and\n+the workflow's worker are two instances of it, and the composition that uses one names it by its\n+member field.\n+\n+<!-- authoring: worker -->\n+```lean\n+/-! ### Entities and domains -/\n+\n+/-- A worker is named by the task queue it polls: the handler's worker and the workflow's worker are\n+two instances of this entity, told apart by their queue. -/\n+entity worker\n+  key: taskQueue\n+\n+enum Phase\n+  | polling\n+  | stopped\n+\n+structure WorkerState where\n+  phase : Phase\n+  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n+\n+enum WorkerOutcome\n+  | accepted\n+\n+/-- A worker records nothing of its own: its stop and resume are faults the Run records against no\n+entity, and what it serves is recorded by the work it serves. -/\n+inductive WorkerFact\n+  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n+\n+/-! ### The actions\n+\n+The two faults are the `worker` party's and name no entity, as the outage machine spells them. The\n+serve action is the worker's own and takes no input, so a composition may synchronize it with an\n+action of any class. -/\n+\n+action workerStop\n+  party: worker\n+\n+action workerResume\n+  party: worker\n+\n+action serve\n+  party: worker\n+  on: worker\n+```\n+\n+The machine starts polling and may end in either phase, because a worker has no natural end. A\n+polling worker serves; a stopped one has no `serve` row, which is the whole point of composing it.\n+\n+<!-- authoring: polling -->\n+```lean\n+/-! ### The machine -/\n+\n+/-- A polling worker stops; a stopped one has nothing to stop. -/\n+def stopStep (state : WorkerState) :\n+    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n+  if state.phase != .polling then [] else\n+  [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .stopped }, facts := [] }]\n+\n+/-- A stopped worker resumes polling; a polling one has nothing to resume. -/\n+def resumeStep (state : WorkerState) :\n+    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n+  if state.phase != .stopped then [] else\n+  [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .polling }, facts := [] }]\n+\n+/-- A polling worker serves and keeps polling; a stopped one serves nothing. -/\n+def serveStep (state : WorkerState) :\n+    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n+  if state.phase != .polling then [] else\n+  [{ outcome := .accepted, state, facts := [] }]\n+\n+/-- A worker has no natural end: it may be left polling or stopped. -/\n+machine polling\n+  for: worker\n+  state: WorkerState\n+  starts: [polling]\n+  ends: [polling, stopped]\n+  steps:\n+    workerStop: stopStep\n+    workerResume: resumeStep\n+    serve: serveStep\n+```\n+\n+An entity or action name is declared once across the production modules under\n+`Temporal.Feature`: `make lint-model` fails a second declaration and names both modules\n+(`feature-entity-uniqueness`, drafted as MOD-18). Its allowlist, in `model/ModelLint/Entity.lean`,\n+holds the duplicates that remain once the worker module exists (`workflow` in the caller, Start and\n+Outage modules, `startWorkflow` in Start and Outage, `workerStop` in the caller, Outage and worker\n+modules, `workerResume` in Outage and worker), each removed by the follow-up that lets a use case\n+choose its entity key and moves the operation entity. Test modules and the success specimen are\n+out of scope.\n+\n+**A second composition.** The Outage Model\n+([`Temporal/Feature/Workflow/Outage/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Workflow/Outage/Model.lean))\n+composes its unchanged machine with the full `polling` machine. All three worker actions are\n+synchronized: the two faults are one step of the workflow's stutter row and the worker's phase\n+change, and the wait for completion is the worker serving. The claim fixes the one composed state a\n+completion leaves, so it holds as a whole-state requirement; the caller's claim in section 12 fixes\n+one member's field while the other varies, which is the field-addressed requirement.\n+\n+<!-- authoring: outage -->\n+```lean\n+/-! ### The workflow and its worker\n+\n+The outage machine composed with the worker of the workflow's task queue. The two faults are one\n+step of both: the workflow's stutter row and the worker's phase change. The wait is the worker\n+serving the workflow task, so it has a row only while the worker polls, and the workflow completes\n+in no state where the worker is stopped. The functional set stays over the machine alone; the\n+composition is what the cross-entity claim is verified over. -/\n+\n+structure WorkerOutageState where\n+  workflow : OutageState\n+  worker : Worker.WorkerState\n+  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr\n+\n+compose workerOutage\n+  for: [workflow, Worker.worker]\n+  state: WorkerOutageState\n+  members:\n+    workflow: workflowOutage\n+    worker: Worker.polling\n+  sync:\n+    workerStop: workflow.workerStop ∥ worker.workerStop\n+    workerResume: workflow.workerResume ∥ worker.workerResume\n+    awaitCompletion: workflow.awaitCompletion ∥ worker.serve\n+  starts: [workflow.pending, worker.polling]\n+  ends: [workflow.completed]\n+\n+/- A completion leaves the one composed state in which the workflow is completed and its worker is\n+polling, and records the completed event: no workflow completes while its worker is stopped. -/\n+property completedByPollingWorker\n+  machine: workerOutage\n+  when: awaitCompletion\n+  holds: fun step =>\n+    step.state.workflow.phase == .completed && step.state.worker.phase == .polling &&\n+      step.facts.contains (.workflow .workflowExecutionCompleted)\n+\n+/- The outage over the composition: the stop and the resume move the worker, and the wait is\n+served by the resumed worker. -/\n+scenario outageServed\n+  model: workerOutage\n+  starts: workflow.pending\n+  actions: [workerStop, workflow.startWorkflow, workerResume, awaitCompletion]\n+\n+query stoppedWorkerCompletesNothing\n+  verify: completedByPollingWorker\n+  in: outageServed\n+  limits: four\n+```\n+\n+What `compose` generates, for both compositions:\n+\n+- `workerOutage.Action`, `.Outcome` and `.Fact`, tagged unions with one constructor per member\n+  wrapping that member's type, which is why a predicate writes\n+  `step.facts.contains (.workflow .workflowExecutionCompleted)`.\n+- Catalog keys a fixture and a Definition ID can carry: a composed state `_`-joins its members'\n+  state keys in field order (`completed_polling`), an unsynchronized action is `<field>_<key>`\n+  (`workflow_startWorkflow`), and a synchronized action is its `sync:` name. Member timers,\n+  unobservable entries and evidence lines are lifted under the same `<field>_<key>` spelling. A\n+  member's state fields are lowered as `<field>_<memberField>` (`<field>` for a one-field member),\n+  which is what a field-addressed requirement fixes. Definition IDs hang off the owner\n+  `compose-<name>` under the file's namespace.\n+- The table of reachable rows only: a breadth-first walk from the starts over the members' tables,\n+  every catalog and each row's results sorted by the lowered order key, emitted as a literal. The\n+  walk refuses a composition whose reachable states times actions exceed the enumeration bound,\n+  with both counts. `workerOutage` reaches 6 states; `nexusCaller` reaches 316 and 1468 rows.\n+- `<name>.agrees`, a theorem decided by `decide +kernel` that the literal agrees with the\n+  composition of the members' tables over every state the starts reach: rows grouped by source\n+  state, each member table read once. A literal the kernel refuses is a located error. On\n+  `nexusCaller` the decisions take 56.5 s of kernel time and the whole caller Model file 241 s at\n+  6.8 GB, which is the cost of composing a 158-state machine.\n+\n+A step of an unsynchronized action moves its member and leaves the others; a `sync:` step is\n+enabled only where every participant has a row, and its results are the ordered product of theirs.\n+Two members owning an action of one name that no `sync:` line pairs is a located error, and so is a\n+`sync:` line naming a timer. The composition carries no `refines:` and no abstract field. No set\n+names it: `set` over a composition is refused, so its Queries are `verify` Queries, and\n+`Selection.select` runs both shipped ones on `veil` with reason `default`.\n+\n+**A derived machine.** A `machine` with `from:` takes its source's entity, state type, starts, ends,\n+setup and evidence, and its rows through the source's own step functions; it carries neither\n+`refines:` nor the abstract field, and it generates its own Action catalog, so its Definition IDs\n+are its own. `restrict: [actions]` keeps the listed actions' rows and drops the rest, every timer\n+and unobservable entry, and every evidence line for a fact no kept row records (section 12's\n+`handlerWorker`). `extend:` adds an author's results to an action's rows, sorted into the step order\n+key; a result where the source has no row, or one the source already returns, is a located error.\n+Both may appear on one machine, and `restrict:` applies first. The negative control\n+([`Temporal/Feature/Nexus/Control/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Nexus/Control/Model.lean)) is the\n+pair machine plus one forged result, so one function is its only step code, and its catalogs, its\n+Case fixture and its recorded Run are the ones the copied machine had.\n+\n+<!-- authoring: derived -->\n+```lean\n+/-! ### The machine\n+\n+The pair Model's machine -- the operation without its deadlines and retries -- plus the forged row.\n+The machine is derived from it rather than copied: every row is the pair machine's, and the one\n+function written here returns the one result the platform never takes. -/\n+\n+/-- The forged row: the non-retryable error completes the operation and records the completed\n+event. The platform's row for that error, which fails the operation and records the failed event,\n+is the pair machine's and stays beside it. -/\n+def controlForgedStep (state : PairState) (reply : Reply) :\n+    List (Step PairState PairOutcome PairFact) :=\n+  if state.phase != .scheduled then [] else\n+  match reply with\n+  | .handlerError false =>\n+      [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .succeeded },\n+         facts := [.nexusOperationCompleted] }]\n+  | _ => []\n+\n+machine nexusControl\n+  from: pair\n+  extend:\n+    handlerReply: controlForgedStep\n+```\n+\n+## 14. A feature Model has one authoring path: the commands. `entity`, `enum` and\n `action` declare its vocabulary, `machine` enumerates its step functions into the finite table,\n `property`, `scenario`, `limits` and `query` say what it promises and what a Query asks, and `set`\n with the platform's `case … realizes` block produces its Cases. Section 4's three \"yes\" rows are later additions.\n \n ## 6. Decisions\n \n Recorded 2026-09-10 with the user; items marked *revised* changed in the simplification pass, and\n fn-85 delivered them as revised. Where delivery departed from a decision, the dated `.N` amendment\n under the section it concerns says how.\n \n 1. **A party is fixed on the action; a set binds it.** *Revised:* the bindings are `driven` and\n    `observed` (previously `test` and `environment`, which collided with a party named\n    `environment`), and `system` is the only reserved party. Timers are `system` behavior written as\n    `after(<timer>)` in rows; faults are actions of a declared party.\n 2. **The author claims classes; exploration owns the evidence.** A class is an enum constructor.\n    Single-value classes need no evidence; a multi-value class is a claim recorded in Provenance with\n    its example (*revised* word, previously \"representative\"), and exploration tries its other\n    members.\n 3. **Protobuf descriptors stay Umpire's only schema.** *Revised:* actions have no `kind`. Whether an\n    action is a call, a command or a reply changed nothing the verifier does, so the distinction stays\n    in the realization, where `Umpire.Operation`'s `unaryRpc` and `sdkCommand` live on. `event`\n    becomes the observation catalog. The schema line is optional.\n 4. **One machine by default; a product machine when needed.** A protocol machine `refines:` a\n    product machine through a state `map:` that defaults by name; steps are derived. It is a\n    refinement, not an Implementation Link.\n+   *Amended during fn-92, 2026-09-29:* machines of different entities compose instead: `compose`\n+   pairs their actions with `sync:` lines into one Model for a cross-entity claim, and a machine\n+   that differs from another by rows is derived with `from:` plus `restrict:` or `extend:` rather\n+   than copied, as the negative control is.\n 5. **One Model for HSM and CHASM, run under both.** Deliberate config-controlled differences are\n    setup parameters; the rollout switch is a set's `repeat`; storage layout is a white-box Known Gap.\n 6. **Words.** `action`, `observation`, `machine`, `refines`, `examples`, `driven`, `observed`.\n    `interface` was rejected as a method-set contract type duplicating Action; `model` and\n    `statemachine` because Model is the checked behavior and Machine the glossary's transition\n    relation; \"mechanism machine\" because MOD-02 gives implementation mechanisms to\n    `Temporal.System`; a top-level `link` because SEM-08 reserves Implementation Link.\n 7. *New:* **State belongs to machines, not entities.** An entity declares identity, references and a\n    key; each machine declares the state it keeps, so the product and protocol machines can track the\n    same entity differently.\n 8. *New:* **Event observations are not declared.** Evidence names resolve against the realization's\n    catalog; only derived observations such as reads are declared.\n 9. *New:* **Validation is rows, not a separate `rules:` block.** Rejections are the first rows of a\n    machine, using the existing first-match order. Constant entity traits (`vary:`) are setup\n    parameters. A machine names its `ends:`, which rows and Properties read as `terminal`.\n 10. *New, needs a rule amendment:* **The realization lives in `Temporal.Case`**, beside the templates\n     it replaces, because MOD-10 forbids `Temporal.System` from importing Feature machines; MOD-02\n     lists Evidence mappings under `Temporal.System` and needs amending to allow it.\n 11. *New:* **Worker instructions carry API messages.** A workflow command carries its\n     `temporal.api.command.v1` attributes and a handler reply its `temporal.api.nexus.v1` message, so\n     the action's schema and its instruction are the same type and no Testpilot field is added per\n     server option. SDK-only options go in an extension field beside the message.\n \n ## Appendix A. A polling worker serves its queue; the `worker` party stops it and resumes it.\n The machine is the one a composition synchronizes with: a workflow or an operation whose progress\n needs a worker names `serve` beside its own action, and a stopped worker has no row for it.\n \n The module declares no set, Case, or Query: nothing here is realized on its own, and the Properties\n about a worker are the cross-entity ones a composition states.\n -/\n \n namespace Temporal.Feature.Worker\n \n open Umpire\n open Umpire.Command\n \n+-- authoring: worker\n+\n /-! ### Entities and domains -/\n \n /-- A worker is named by the task queue it polls: the handler's worker and the workflow's worker are\n two instances of this entity, told apart by their queue. -/\n entity worker\n   key: taskQueue\n \n enum Phase\n   | polling\n   | stopped\n \n structure WorkerState where\n   phase : Phase\n   deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n \n enum WorkerOutcome\n   | accepted\n \n /-- A worker records nothing of its own: its stop and resume are faults the Run records against no\n entity, and what it serves is recorded by the work it serves. -/\n inductive WorkerFact\n   deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n \n /-! ### The actions\n \n The two faults are the `worker` party's and name no entity, as the outage machine spells them. Before a start, the server records a Started event first, which is\nwhy the evidence is two facts and not one -- and why the product machine, which has no `backingOff`\nphase to have skipped, could write the completion alone. -/\ndef protocolCompleteStep (state : ProtocolState) (resolution : Resolution) :\n    List (Step ProtocolState ProtocolOutcome ProtocolFact) :=\n  if terminalPhase state.phase then\n    [{ outcome := .notFound, state, facts := [] }]\n  else if state.phase == .unscheduled then []\n  else\n    let startedFirst : List ProtocolFact :=\n      if state.phase == .started then [] else [.nexusOperationStarted]\n    match resolution with\n    | .succeeded => moves state .succeeded (startedFirst ++ [.nexusOperationCompleted])\n    | .failed => moves state .failed (startedFirst ++ [.nexusOperationFailed])\n    | .canceled => moves state .canceled (startedFirst ++ [.nexusOperationCanceled])\n\n/-- The backoff timer. A phase of the same name is that phase; backing\noff is still scheduled, because the product machine cannot see a retry; and an operation not yet\nscheduled reads as scheduled, because the product machine begins there. Every other field is\nhidden, which is what a map that does not read it says. -/\ndef productOf (state : ProtocolState) : ProductState :=\n  { phase := match state.phase with\n    | .unscheduled | .scheduled | .backingOff => .scheduled\n    | .started => .started\n    | .succeeded => .succeeded\n    | .failed => .failed\n    | .canceled => .canceled\n    | .timedOut => .timedOut }\n\nmachine nexusProtocol\n  for: operation\n  state: ProtocolState\n  refines: nexusProduct\n  map: productOf\n  starts: [unscheduled]\n  ends: [succeeded, failed, canceled, timedOut]\n  timers: [backoff, scheduleToClose, scheduleToStart, startToClose]\n  unobservable: [backoff]\n  evidence:\n    nexusOperationScheduled: nexusOperationScheduled\n    nexusOperationStarted: nexusOperationStarted\n    nexusOperationCompleted: nexusOperationCompleted\n    nexusOperationFailed: nexusOperationFailed\n    nexusOperationCanceled: nexusOperationCanceled\n    nexusOperationTimedOut: nexusOperationTimedOut\n    pendingAttempts: pendingAttempts\n  steps:\n    schedule: scheduleStep\n    handlerReply: protocolHandlerReplyStep\n    complete: protocolCompleteStep\n    transportFault: protocolTransportFaultStep\n    workerStop: protocolWorkerStopStep\n    backoff: backoffStep\n    scheduleToClose: scheduleToCloseStep\n    scheduleToStart: scheduleToStartStep\n    startToClose: startToCloseStep\n```\n\n## 7. What the machines promise\n\nA `property` is an ordinary Lean predicate the command enumerates over the machine's table. A\nsame-step claim names its action under `when:` and holds of the step that action produces; a\ntransition claim holds of the step before and the step after. A same-step claim fixes one state\nor none, which is what lets a functional Case lower it to a Contract clause triggered by the\naction the Case performs.\n\n<!-- authoring: properties -->\n```lean\n/-! ### What the machines promise\n\nA same-step claim names the action it is about under `when:` and holds of the step that action\nproduces; a transition claim holds of the step before and the step after. A functional Query\nrealizes a same-step claim, because the Case's Contract is the claim's clause triggered by the\naction the Case performs; a transition claim is searched and verified, never realized. -/\n\n/- Once an operation is over, no step changes its phase. Declared on the product machine and read\non the protocol machine through the map. -/\nproperty terminalIsFinal\n  machine: nexusProduct\n  holds: fun before after =>\n    !(productTerminal before.state) || after.state.phase == before.state.phase\n\n/- A synchronous reply settles the operation as succeeded, and the completed event records it. -/\nproperty syncSucceeds\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: handlerReply (syncSuccess)\n  holds: fun step =>\n    step.state.phase == .succeeded && step.facts.contains .nexusOperationCompleted\n\n/- An asynchronous reply starts the operation, and the started event records it. -/\nproperty asyncStarts\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: handlerReply (async)\n  holds: fun step => step.state.phase == .started && step.facts.contains .nexusOperationStarted\n\n/- A successful completion is recorded by the completed event. Neither the phase nor the outcome\nis fixed: a completion resolves any running phase, and `accepted` is every earlier step's outcome\ntoo, so a clause fixing it would be answered before the completion. -/\nproperty completionSucceeds\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: complete (succeeded)\n  holds: fun step => step.facts.contains .nexusOperationCompleted\n\n/- A failed completion is recorded by the failed event. -/\nproperty completionFails\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: complete (failed)\n  holds: fun step => step.facts.contains .nexusOperationFailed\n\n/- A non-retryable handler error settles the operation as failed, and the failed event records it. -/\nproperty handlerErrorFails\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: handlerReply (handlerError false)\n  holds: fun step => step.state.phase == .failed && step.facts.contains .nexusOperationFailed\n\n/-- Succeeded on the second attempt of an operation with no deadline set. A claim fixes one state, so\nevery field is named. -/\ndef succeededOnRetry : ProtocolState :=\n  { phase := .succeeded, attempts := 1, scheduleToClose := .unset, scheduleToStart := .unset,\n    startToClose := .unset }\n\n/- A synchronous reply to the retried attempt settles the operation as succeeded on its second\nattempt: the count the retryable failure raised is still one, and the completed event records the\nreply. -/\nproperty retrySucceeds\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: handlerReply (syncSuccess)\n  holds: fun step =>\n    step.state == succeededOnRetry && step.facts.contains .nexusOperationCompleted\n\n/- The schedule-to-start deadline settles an operation no handler started as timed out, and the\ntimed-out event records which deadline it was. -/\nproperty scheduleToStartFires\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: scheduleToStart\n  holds: fun step =>\n    step.state.phase == .timedOut &&\n      step.facts.contains (.nexusOperationTimedOut (timeoutType := .scheduleToStart))\n\n/- The start-to-close deadline settles a started operation no handler completed as timed out. -/\nproperty startToCloseFires\n  machine: nexusProtocol\n  when: startToClose\n  holds: fun step =>\n    step.state.phase == .timedOut &&\n      step.facts.contains (.nexusOperationTimedOut (timeoutType := .startToClose))\n```\n\n## 8. The paths and their limits\n\nA `scenario` is one path: the classed actions in order from a starting phase. Each path below is\none upstream functional test's shape. `limits` bound the search that finds the path's witness.\n\n`steps` and `actions` bound the length of a trace, and the search explores to the smaller of the\ntwo. What `search` counts depends on the backend. The frozen `reference` backend enumerates\ncandidate paths, so `search` bounds paths and a state reached along two paths counts twice. The\n`veil` backend, Veil's concrete checker over the product of the Model state, the Scenario's\nprogress and the Property monitors, visits each product state once, so `search` bounds product\nstates. A Query runs on `veil` unless its strategy is `seeded` or its Property or Scenario uses a\nconstruct no monitor or progress automaton encodes yet; then it runs on `reference`. The planning\nreceipt records the backend, the reason and the unit.\n\nThe two backends earn trust differently. On either one, a witness counts only after it is replayed\nagainst the Model's checked table. An absence answer (`verified-within-limits`, `none-found`,\n`unsatisfiable`) is search within the Limits, not a kernel proof. On `veil` it comes from the pinned\nVeil checker's breadth-first step, which the search runs as an ordinary pure function, and rests\non the adapter's theorems that Veil's transition system is the product's own relation, on\ndeduplication that compares whole product states rather than hashes, and on the differential test,\nwhich runs every checked-in Query on both backends and requires the same answer wherever\n`reference` finishes within its Limits.\n\n<!-- authoring: scenarios -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The paths the Queries run\n\nA protocol Scenario names its classed actions with their inputs and its start by its phase. Each\npath below is one upstream functional test's shape: the schedule command with no deadline set,\nthen the side effects that settle the operation. -/\n\nscenario syncReplied\n  model: nexusProtocol\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, unset, unset), handlerReply (syncSuccess)]\n\nscenario asyncThenSucceeded\n  model: nexusProtocol\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, unset, unset), handlerReply (async), complete (succeeded)]\n\nscenario asyncThenFailed\n  model: nexusProtocol\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, unset, unset), handlerReply (async), complete (failed)]\n\nscenario nonRetryableError\n  model: nexusProtocol\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, unset, unset), handlerReply (handlerError false)]\n\n/- The retryable error backs the operation off; the backoff timer fires and records nothing; the\nretried attempt is answered synchronously. -/\nscenario retriedThenSucceeded\n  model: nexusProtocol\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, unset, unset), handlerReply (handlerError true), backoff,\n    handlerReply (syncSuccess)]\n\n/- The schedule command sets the schedule-to-start deadline; the handler's worker stops, so nothing\nanswers the start request; the deadline fires. The worker stops after the schedule in the\noperation's order, where the stop changes nothing; the realization stops it before the workflow\nstarts, where the stop cannot race the dispatch. -/\nscenario scheduleToStartExpires\n  model: nexusProtocol\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, expires, unset), workerStop, scheduleToStart]\n\n/- The schedule command sets the start-to-close deadline; the handler accepts asynchronously and\nnever completes; the deadline fires. -/\nscenario startToCloseExpires\n  model: nexusProtocol\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, unset, expires), handlerReply (async), startToClose]\n\n/- Nine actions are enabled before the operation is scheduled and eleven once it is, so an exact\nsequence of two is found among ninety-nine candidates, one of three among about a thousand and one\nof four among about ten thousand. -/\nlimits two\n  steps: 2\n  actions: 2\n  search: 512\n\nlimits three\n  steps: 3\n  actions: 3\nimport Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Pair.Model\n\n/-!\n# The negative control\n\nA Model whose one claim the platform contradicts, on purpose. It is the caller Model's `operation`\nentity and action classes over a machine that keeps the real rows of every reply and adds one row\nthe platform never takes: a non-retryable handler error completing the operation as succeeded,\nwith the completed event recording it. Its one Query selects that row and its Property names it, so\nthe Case the caller realization produces expects a completed event where the platform writes a\nfailed one, and every Run of it is violated, deterministically and honestly.\n\nThat is what a replay needs before any reducer exists: one Case whose Run is violated and can be\nrecorded, admitted, replayed offline and rerun. The control enters no functional, canary or\nexploratory set of the caller Model and no regression view; its Case is registered under\n`temporal.case.nexusCallerControl.<query>` so that `umpire-case` renders its fixture and the live\nsuite runs it, and nothing reads it as a claim about the platform. The proposal a replay compiles\nfrom it proves the promotion mechanism only: its expected trace is the row the platform never\ntakes, and it is never reviewed into a regression set.\n\nThe forged row is a second result of a row the platform has, not a replaced one: the Case Runtime\nadmits a recorded step only as an authorized row, and a rule is violated only when the observed\nstep is such a row whose response fails. The real row stays authorized, and since the Producer\ndeclares the evidence of every result of a witnessed row, the failed event the platform writes is\nlifted and projected to the real row rather than left unread.\n-/\n\nnamespace Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Control\n\nopen Umpire\nopen Umpire.Command\nopen Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Caller\nopen Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Pair\n\n-- authoring: derived\n\n/-! ### The machine\n\nThe pair Model's machine -- the operation without its deadlines and retries -- plus the forged row.\nThe machine is derived from it rather than copied: every row is the pair machine's, and the one\nfunction written here returns the one result the platform never takes. -/\n\n/-- The forged row: the non-retryable error completes the operation and records the completed\nevent. The platform's row for that error, which fails the operation and records the failed event,\nis the pair machine's and stays beside it. -/\ndef controlForgedStep (state : PairState) (reply : Reply) :\n    List (Step PairState PairOutcome PairFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .scheduled then [] else\n  match reply with\n  | .handlerError false =>\n      [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .succeeded },\n         facts := [.nexusOperationCompleted] }]\n  | _ => []\n\nmachine nexusControl\n  from: pair\n  extend:\n    handlerReply: controlForgedStep\n\n-- authoring: end\n\n/-! ### The claim the platform contradicts -/\n\n/- A non-retryable handler error completes the operation, and the completed event records it. The\nplatform fails it instead, so a Run of a Case realizing this claim is violated. -/\nproperty forgedSuccess\n  machine: nexusControl\n  when: handlerReply (handlerError false)\n  holds: fun step =>\n    step.state.phase == .succeeded && step.facts.contains .nexusOperationCompleted\n\n/-! ### The path, the Query, the set and the Case -/\n\nscenario nonRetryableErrorForged\n  model: nexusControl\n  starts: unscheduled\n  actions: [schedule (unset, unset, unset), handlerReply (handlerError false)]\n\nlimits controlTwo\n  steps: 2\n  actions: 2\n  search: 16\n\nquery forgedCompletion\n  find: forgedSuccess\n  in: nonRetryableErrorForged\n  limits: controlTwo\n\nset nexusCallerControl\n  purpose: functional\n  bind:\n    caller: driven\n    handler: driven\n  queries: [forgedCompletion]\n\n/- The caller realization serves the control: the schedule and the handler's error reply are the\nclasses it binds, and the Producer declares the failed event's evidence beside the completed one's\nfor the witnessed row. -/\ncase nexusCallerControlCases\n  realizes nexusCallerControl\n  as nexusCallerCases.realization\n\nend Temporal.Feature.Nexus.Control\n.plans/UMPIRE4_SPEC.md:225:  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; A composition over the operation and its worker\n\nA `compose` block builds one Model from machines of different entities, for a claim no one of them\ncan state. The protocol machine cannot see the handler's worker: its `workerStop` is a stutter row.\nComposed with the worker entity's machine, restricted by `restrict:` to the actions the caller's\nview needs, each `sync:` line makes one step of both members, so a reply is enabled only where the\nworker's `serve` has a row, which is only while it polls. The composed state is an author-written\nstructure with one field per member, and a Property reads one member's field while the other\nvaries. Scenario and Property names resolve member-qualified actions (`operation.schedule`) and\n`sync:` names (`handlerReply`); no set names a composition, so its Query is a `verify` Query.\n\n<!-- authoring: composition -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The operation and the handler's worker\n\nThe protocol machine's worker stop is a stutter row: the operation cannot see its handler's worker,\nso the schedule-to-start Scenario orders the stop before the request by convention. Composed with\nthe worker of the handler's task queue, the stop is the worker's own phase change and every reply\nis the worker serving, so a reply has a row only while the worker polls. No set names the\ncomposition; it is what the cross-entity claim is verified over. -/\n\n/-- The caller's view of the handler's worker: it stops and it serves. It never resumes, because an\naction no `sync:` line names would stay executable on its own and admit a stop, a resume and then a\nreply; the operation's timers settle every state a stop leaves. -/\nmachine handlerWorker\n  from: Worker.polling\n  restrict: [workerStop, serve]\n\nstructure NexusCallerState where\n  operation : ProtocolState\n  worker : Worker.WorkerState\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr\n\ncompose nexusCaller\n  for: [operation, Worker.worker]\n  state: NexusCallerState\n  members:\n    operation: nexusProtocol\n    worker: handlerWorker\n  sync:\n    workerStop: operation.workerStop ∥ worker.workerStop\n    handlerReply: operation.handlerReply ∥ worker.serve\n  starts: [operation.unscheduled, worker.polling]\n  ends: [operation.succeeded, operation.failed, operation.canceled, operation.timedOut]\n\n/- Every reply, of any class, leaves the handler's worker polling: no handler replies while its\nworker is stopped. -/\nproperty repliedByPollingWorker\n  machine: nexusCaller\n  when: handlerReply\n  holds: fun step => step.state.worker.phase == .polling\n\n/- A retryable reply backs the operation off; the handler's worker then stops, so the retried\nattempt is never answered and the schedule-to-start deadline fires. -/\nscenario repliedThenStopped\n  model: nexusCaller\n  starts: operation.unscheduled\n  actions: [operation.schedule (unset, expires, unset), handlerReply (handlerError true),\n    workerStop, operation.scheduleToStart]\n\nquery stoppedWorkerRepliesNothing\n  verify: repliedByPollingWorker\n  in: repliedThenStopped\n  limits: four\n```\n\n## 13. The worker entity, a second composition, and a derived machine\n\nSection 12's composition names the worker entity's module. This section quotes that module, the\nOutage Model's composition and the derived negative control, and the drift test checks their marked\nregions as it checks this file's: a region name is unique across every file the walkthrough quotes,\nand each file closes its last quoted region with its own `-- authoring: end`.\n\n**An entity module.** [`Temporal/Feature/Worker/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Worker/Model.lean)\ndeclares one entity, the worker of a task queue, and no set, Case or Query: nothing in it is\nrealized on its own. Its machine is what a composition synchronizes with. The handler's worker and\nthe workflow's worker are two instances of it, and the composition that uses one names it by its\nmember field.\n\n<!-- authoring: worker -->\n```lean\n/-! ### Entities and domains -/\n\n/-- A worker is named by the task queue it polls: the handler's worker and the workflow's worker are\ntwo instances of this entity, told apart by their queue. -/\nentity worker\n  key: taskQueue\n\nenum Phase\n  | polling\n  | stopped\n\nstructure WorkerState where\n  phase : Phase\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n\nenum WorkerOutcome\n  | accepted\n\n/-- A worker records nothing of its own: its stop and resume are faults the Run records against no\nentity, and what it serves is recorded by the work it serves. -/\ninductive WorkerFact\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr, Finite\n\n/-! ### The actions\n\nThe two faults are the `worker` party's and name no entity, as the outage machine spells them. The\nserve action is the worker's own and takes no input, so a composition may synchronize it with an\naction of any class. -/\n\naction workerStop\n  party: worker\n\naction workerResume\n  party: worker\n\naction serve\n  party: worker\n  on: worker\n```\n\nThe machine starts polling and may end in either phase, because a worker has no natural end. A\npolling worker serves; a stopped one has no `serve` row, which is the whole point of composing it.\n\n<!-- authoring: polling -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The machine -/\n\n/-- A polling worker stops; a stopped one has nothing to stop. -/\ndef stopStep (state : WorkerState) :\n    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .polling then [] else\n  [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .stopped }, facts := [] }]\n\n/-- A stopped worker resumes polling; a polling one has nothing to resume. -/\ndef resumeStep (state : WorkerState) :\n    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .stopped then [] else\n  [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .polling }, facts := [] }]\n\n/-- A polling worker serves and keeps polling; a stopped one serves nothing. -/\ndef serveStep (state : WorkerState) :\n    List (Step WorkerState WorkerOutcome WorkerFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .polling then [] else\n  [{ outcome := .accepted, state, facts := [] }]\n\n/-- A worker has no natural end: it may be left polling or stopped. -/\nmachine polling\n  for: worker\n  state: WorkerState\n  starts: [polling]\n  ends: [polling, stopped]\n  steps:\n    workerStop: stopStep\n    workerResume: resumeStep\n    serve: serveStep\n```\n\nAn entity or action name is declared once across the production modules under\n`Temporal.Feature`: `make lint-model` fails a second declaration and names both modules\n(`feature-entity-uniqueness`, drafted as MOD-18). Its allowlist, in `model/ModelLint/Entity.lean`,\nholds the duplicates that remain once the worker module exists (`workflow` in the caller, Start and\nOutage modules, `startWorkflow` in Start and Outage, `workerStop` in the caller, Outage and worker\nmodules, `workerResume` in Outage and worker), each removed by the follow-up that lets a use case\nchoose its entity key and moves the operation entity. Test modules and the success specimen are\nout of scope.\n\n**A second composition.** The Outage Model\n([`Temporal/Feature/Workflow/Outage/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Workflow/Outage/Model.lean))\ncomposes its unchanged machine with the full `polling` machine. All three worker actions are\nsynchronized: the two faults are one step of the workflow's stutter row and the worker's phase\nchange, and the wait for completion is the worker serving. The claim fixes the one composed state a\ncompletion leaves, so it holds as a whole-state requirement; the caller's claim in section 12 fixes\none member's field while the other varies, which is the field-addressed requirement.\n\n<!-- authoring: outage -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The workflow and its worker\n\nThe outage machine composed with the worker of the workflow's task queue. The two faults are one\nstep of both: the workflow's stutter row and the worker's phase change. The wait is the worker\nserving the workflow task, so it has a row only while the worker polls, and the workflow completes\nin no state where the worker is stopped. The functional set stays over the machine alone; the\ncomposition is what the cross-entity claim is verified over. -/\n\nstructure WorkerOutageState where\n  workflow : OutageState\n  worker : Worker.WorkerState\n  deriving BEq, DecidableEq, Repr\n\ncompose workerOutage\n  for: [workflow, Worker.worker]\n  state: WorkerOutageState\n  members:\n    workflow: workflowOutage\n    worker: Worker.polling\n  sync:\n    workerStop: workflow.workerStop ∥ worker.workerStop\n    workerResume: workflow.workerResume ∥ worker.workerResume\n    awaitCompletion: workflow.awaitCompletion ∥ worker.serve\n  starts: [workflow.pending, worker.polling]\n  ends: [workflow.completed]\n\n/- A completion leaves the one composed state in which the workflow is completed and its worker is\npolling, and records the completed event: no workflow completes while its worker is stopped. -/\nproperty completedByPollingWorker\n  machine: workerOutage\n  when: awaitCompletion\n  holds: fun step =>\n    step.state.workflow.phase == .completed && step.state.worker.phase == .polling &&\n      step.facts.contains (.workflow .workflowExecutionCompleted)\n\n/- The outage over the composition: the stop and the resume move the worker, and the wait is\nserved by the resumed worker. -/\nscenario outageServed\n  model: workerOutage\n  starts: workflow.pending\n  actions: [workerStop, workflow.startWorkflow, workerResume, awaitCompletion]\n\nquery stoppedWorkerCompletesNothing\n  verify: completedByPollingWorker\n  in: outageServed\n  limits: four\n```\n\nWhat `compose` generates, for both compositions:\n\n- `workerOutage.Action`, `.Outcome` and `.Fact`, tagged unions with one constructor per member\n  wrapping that member's type, which is why a predicate writes\n  `step.facts.contains (.workflow .workflowExecutionCompleted)`.\n- Catalog keys a fixture and a Definition ID can carry: a composed state `_`-joins its members'\n  state keys in field order (`completed_polling`), an unsynchronized action is `<field>_<key>`\n  (`workflow_startWorkflow`), and a synchronized action is its `sync:` name. Member timers,\n  unobservable entries and evidence lines are lifted under the same `<field>_<key>` spelling. A\n  member's state fields are lowered as `<field>_<memberField>` (`<field>` for a one-field member),\n  which is what a field-addressed requirement fixes. Definition IDs hang off the owner\n  `compose-<name>` under the file's namespace.\n- The table of reachable rows only: a breadth-first walk from the starts over the members' tables,\n  every catalog and each row's results sorted by the lowered order key, emitted as a literal. The\n  walk refuses a composition whose reachable states times actions exceed the enumeration bound,\n  with both counts. `workerOutage` reaches 6 states; `nexusCaller` reaches 316 and 1468 rows.\n- `<name>.agrees`, a theorem decided by `decide +kernel` that the literal agrees with the\n  composition of the members' tables over every state the starts reach: rows grouped by source\n  state, each member table read once. A literal the kernel refuses is a located error. On\n  `nexusCaller` the decisions take 56.5 s of kernel time and the whole caller Model file 241 s at\n  6.8 GB, which is the cost of composing a 158-state machine.\n\nA step of an unsynchronized action moves its member and leaves the others; a `sync:` step is\nenabled only where every participant has a row, and its results are the ordered product of theirs.\nTwo members owning an action of one name that no `sync:` line pairs is a located error, and so is a\n`sync:` line naming a timer. The composition carries no `refines:` and no abstract field. No set\nnames it: `set` over a composition is refused, so its Queries are `verify` Queries, and\n`Selection.select` runs both shipped ones on `veil` with reason `default`.\n\n**A derived machine.** A `machine` with `from:` takes its source's entity, state type, starts, ends,\nsetup and evidence, and its rows through the source's own step functions; it carries neither\n`refines:` nor the abstract field, and it generates its own Action catalog, so its Definition IDs\nare its own. `restrict: [actions]` keeps the listed actions' rows and drops the rest, every timer\nand unobservable entry, and every evidence line for a fact no kept row records (section 12's\n`handlerWorker`). `extend:` adds an author's results to an action's rows, sorted into the step order\nkey; a result where the source has no row, or one the source already returns, is a located error.\nBoth may appear on one machine, and `restrict:` applies first. The negative control\n([`Temporal/Feature/Nexus/Control/Model.lean`](Temporal/Feature/Nexus/Control/Model.lean)) is the\npair machine plus one forged result, so one function is its only step code, and its catalogs, its\nCase fixture and its recorded Run are the ones the copied machine had.\n\n<!-- authoring: derived -->\n```lean\n/-! ### The machine\n\nThe pair Model's machine -- the operation without its deadlines and retries -- plus the forged row.\nThe machine is derived from it rather than copied: every row is the pair machine's, and the one\nfunction written here returns the one result the platform never takes. -/\n\n/-- The forged row: the non-retryable error completes the operation and records the completed\nevent. The platform's row for that error, which fails the operation and records the failed event,\nis the pair machine's and stays beside it. -/\ndef controlForgedStep (state : PairState) (reply : Reply) :\n    List (Step PairState PairOutcome PairFact) :=\n  if state.phase != .scheduled then [] else\n  match reply with\n  | .handlerError false =>\n      [{ outcome := .accepted, state := { phase := .succeeded },\n         facts := [.nexusOperationCompleted] }]\n  | _ => []\n\nmachine nexusControl\n  from: pair\n  extend:\n    handlerReply: controlForgedStep\n```\n\n## 14. From the file to a green live test\n\n```sh\ncd model && lake build                               # the file compiles, or says where it does not\nmake umpire-gen-case-runtime-conformance             # renders one fixture per functional Query\nmake umpire-check-case-runtime-conformance           # the checked-in fixtures are the rendered ones\n```\n\n`umpire-case --list` prints the seven Cases the functional set produces beside the explicitly\nregistered ones. The live test names the fixture and asserts the Verdict; in\n`tests/testpilot_nexus_caller_case_test.go` each Query is one entry of `nexusCallerQueries` (the\nhistory events its Contract's supporting evidence must name) and one function that runs it under\nboth values of the implementation switch:\n\n```sh\nTMPDIR=$(cd /tmp && pwd -P) \\\n  go test -count=1 -tags test_dep,integration ./tests -run TestTestpilotNexusCallerRetry\n```\n\n`make umpire-check-regression` runs every gate: the Lean build, the goldens, the fixtures, the\ninventory, the retired vocabulary and the live suite. A change to a block above is a change to the\nModel file, and the drift test (`go test ./tools/umpire/authoring/...`) says so before the gate\ndoes.\n  and it is the only such surface. Each command MUST elaborate\n  to the ordinary `Umpire.Property`, `Umpire.Scenario` and `Umpire.Query` declarations AUT-07 names,\n  MUST NOT admit anything those languages reject, and MUST NOT define behavior of its own. A\n  command surface that is not `Umpire.Command` is the \"another way to define behavior\" AUT-07\n  forbids. `Umpire.Command` MUST NOT name a feature, a protocol, or a runtime: which Definition ID\n  root a project hangs its families off, which namespace prefix is scaffolding, and what a Case ID\n  is rooted at are the project's declared conventions, not the surface's.\n  *Amendment (drafted by fn-85; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The surface also carries `set`, which\n  groups Queries by purpose and binds parties, and `register_switch`, by which a realization\n  registers the switches a set's `repeat:` names. Producing Cases is not part of the surface: a\n  platform declares its own Case-producing block beside its realizations (`Temporal.Case.Syntax`\n  declares `case … realizes <set> as <realization>`), which names a set and a realization and\n  defines no behavior, so `Umpire.Command` names no feature and the platform's block is where a\n  feature is named.\n  *Amendment (drafted by fn-86; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The command surface is the only\n  authoring path for a feature Model. A production module under `Temporal.Feature` or\n  `Umpire.Examples` declares its entities, domains, actions, machines, Properties, Scenarios,\n  Limits, Queries and sets through the commands and reaches `Umpire.Model`, `Umpire.Property`,\n  `Umpire.Scenario`, `Umpire.Query`, `Umpire.Operation` and `Umpire.Case` only through\n  `Umpire.Command`: a concrete API operation is an `action`'s `schema:` line, a field relation is\n  a `property`'s `relates:` line, and a Case is the platform's `case … realizes` block over a\n  realization. MOD-16 enforces the import boundary.\n  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The surface also carries `compose`,\n  which builds one Model from declared machines, and the `from:`, `restrict:` and `extend:` keys of\n  `machine`, which derive a machine from a declared one. Neither defines behavior of its own: a\n  composed step is its members' rows (an unsynchronized action steps its member alone; a\n  synchronized one is enabled only where every participant has a row, and its results are the\n  product of theirs), and a derived machine's rows are its source's rows, kept or dropped by\n  `restrict:`, plus the results an author's `extend:` function returns. `compose` elaborates to\n  `Umpire.Command.DeclaredModel`, which `property`, `scenario`, `limits` and `query` accept as they\n  accept a machine; a `set` over a composition is refused, because realizing one is outside this\n  surface.\n- **AUT-08 — Finite Model adapter.** Authors SHOULD use the proof-carrying\n  `Umpire.FiniteMachine` adapter when a complete finite Model has enumerators that define its\n  authoritative behavior. The adapter derives membership relations, completeness support, and\n  exact finite Search. Authors MUST still provide ordered semantic domains, encoders, enumerators,\n  evidence that enumerated values stay within those domains, and evidence that every enumerated\n  Action is executable. The path MUST produce an `Umpire.DraftModel` and pass it to\n  `Umpire.checkModel`. `Umpire.FiniteMachine` MUST NOT introduce another Property, Query, Scenario,\n  or macro language.\n  *Amendment (drafted by fn-86; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The expert alternative this rule offered\n  -- constructing `Umpire.Machine` directly for a Model whose authority is specified independently --\n  is withdrawn for feature Models. A Model under `Temporal.Feature` or `Umpire.Examples` is declared\n  through the commands (AUT-07a): `machine` enumerates the author's step functions into the finite\n  table (`Umpire.FiniteTable`) whose kernel is this adapter's, and discharges the adapter's\n  obligations from the table. Direct `Umpire.Machine` and `Umpire.DraftModel` construction remains\n  what `Temporal.System.Nexus.ImplementationLink` and Umpire's own tests do, because they exercise\n  those records; it is not an authoring path for a feature Model, and MOD-16 enforces that at the\n  import.\n- **AUT-09 — Macro-derived finite domains.** *(drafted by fn-80; approved 2026-09-10 under GOV-02.)*\n  AUT-08's \"author-provided\" includes a domain a command macro derives from the author's\n  own declarations: the ordered domains, encoders and enumerators an authoring macro elaborates from\n  the constructors of an enum-like inductive the author named are author-provided, not inferred. The\n  macro MUST derive them from declarations the author wrote and MUST NOT admit a spelling the author\n  did not declare. Completeness, domain membership and Action executability MUST still be discharged\n  against the same authorities AUT-08 names; a macro MUST NOT weaken or assume them.\n  *Amendment (drafted by fn-85; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* Author-provided also covers what the\n  `machine`, `property`, `scenario` and `enum` commands derive: a `structure` of finite fields as\n  the state domain (`Umpire.Command.Finite`), one step function per action enumerated into the\n  finite table in the catalog's order, a `property` predicate enumerated into clause records by\n  probing that table, a refinement decided by the kernel over the two tables\n  (`Umpire.Command.Refinement`), and a Scenario's `instances:` product. Each is derived from\n  declarations the author wrote and admits no spelling the author did not declare; an enumeration\n  past `Umpire.Command.elaborationBound` is refused with the numbers that exceeded it, never\n  truncated.\n  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* Author-provided also covers the\n  reachable domain `compose` computes from the declared member tables: the composed state and action\n  catalogs and the literal table a breadth-first walk from the declared starts reaches. The walk\n  admits no state or action the members' tables do not, a walk past\n  `Umpire.Command.enumerationBound` is refused with both counts, and the kernel decides for each\n  composition by `decide +kernel`, not `native_decide`, that the literal is sound and complete with\n  respect to the members' tables over every reachable state.\n\n## Search, Limits, and Artifacts\n\n### Search and Artifact concepts\n\n- **Limit (`Umpire.Limit`).** A typed ceiling on one stage, in one `Umpire.LimitUnit`: `steps`,\n  `actions`, `logicalTime`, `search`, or `plans`. Rows and\n every array are sorted; empty arrays are present; the document is compact, one line, and ends with\n one LF. It is a navigation aid for a reader about to change a module and carries no Definition ID,\n Artifact Checksum or other semantic claim; a test root on a row says the root imports the module,\n not that its suite ran.\n \n The exporter is opt-in and unversioned: neither Lake's default targets nor version control carry it\n or its output. It refuses to run outside the model package root (it loads the current directory as\n a Lake root and requires the `temporal-model` package owning `umpire-lint`, `umpire-lint-tests` and\n itself; a relocated checkout passes). Success is exactly the document on stdout, status 0 and an\n empty stderr; any inventory, build, metadata, index or root failure is status 1, an empty stdout and\n diagnostics on stderr. The document is complete in memory before the final write, so a failure\n before it leaves nothing on stdout; if the final write itself fails, the status is non-zero and\n whatever the stream had already accepted is not a document. The check target runs the process\n suite (warm, stale and cold Lake paths, a relocated checkout, wrong roots, injected failures) and\n the Make path with each stream captured separately.\n \n ## Superseded runtime history\n \n The pre-fn-64 portable-plan, resident-executor, caller-specific adapter, and separate Run Evaluation\n interfaces were removed. owns it when the document marks it as planned rather than present.\ntype SpecName struct {\n\tName string\n\tLine int\n\t// PlannedSpec is the Flow spec ID from a `(planned: fn-...)` marker in the A block is one list item or\n// one blank-line-separated paragraph, so a rule marked planned covers the names in its\n// own text and no others.\nfunc ExtractSpecNames(document string) []SpecName {\n\tlines := strings.Split(document, \"\\n\")\n\tblockOf := make([]int, len(lines))\n\tblock := 0\n\tpreviousBlank := true\n\tfor index, line := range lines {\n\t\tblank := strings.TrimSpace(line) == \"\"\n\t\tif blockStart.MatchString(line) || (previousBlank && !blank) {\n\t\t\tblock++\n\t\t}\n\t\tblockOf[index] = block\n\t\tpreviousBlank = blank\n\t}\n\n\tplannedOf := map[int]string{}\n\tfor index, line := range lines {\n\t\tif match := plannedMarker.FindStringSubmatch(line); match != nil {\n\t\t\tplannedOf[blockOf[index]] = match[1]\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tvar names []SpecName\n\tfor index, line := range lines {\n\t\tfor _, match := range backticked.FindAllStringSubmatch(line, -1) {\n\t\t\tcandidate := match[1]\n\t\t\tif !modelName.MatchString(candidate) {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tnames = append(names, SpecName{\n\t\t\t\tName:        candidate,\n\t\t\t\tLine:        index + 1,\n\t\t\t\tPlannedSpec: plannedOf[blockOf[index]],\n\t\t\t})\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn names\n}\n\n// Unresolved returns one message per citation a document makes that the tree does not\n// back: a name that is not in the index, or a name marked planned whose owning Flow spec\n// is not open. Messages are sorted, and an empty result means the document's mechanical\n// half holds. `label` prefixes each message, so a caller can name the document.\nfunc Unresolved(index *Index, names []SpecName, label, specsDirectory string) ([]string, error) {\n\topenSpec := map[string]bool{}\n\tvar messages []string\n\tfor _, name := range names {\n\t\t// A name that resolves is accepted whatever its block says. A planned\n// term is only allowed while somebody owns delivering it, so a missing record and a\n// closed one are both refusals rather than errors.\nfunc SpecIsOpen(specsDirectory, specID string) (bool, error) {\n\tpath := filepath.Join(specsDirectory, specID+\".json\")\n\tcontent, err := os.ReadFile(path)\n\tif os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\treturn false, nil\n\t}\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"read %s: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\tvar record struct {\n\t\tStatus string `json:\"status\"`\n\t}\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(content, &record); err != nil {\n\t\treturn false, fmt.Errorf(\"decode %s: %w\", path, err)\n\t}\n\treturn record.Status == \"open\", nil\n}\npackage vocabulary_test\n\nimport (\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"path/filepath\"\n\t\"testing\"\n\n\t\"github.com/stretchr/testify/require\"\n\t\"go.temporal.io/server/tools/umpire/internal/leannames\"\n)\n\n// TestUmpireSpecNamesResolveAgainstTheModelTree checks the mechanical half of the\n// glossary contract: every dotted Lean name the specification cites in backticks names\n// a module, a namespace, or a declaration that exists, unless the rule that cites it is\n// marked planned and the spec that owns delivering it is still open. The `machine` command declares one over a\n   311\t  `structure` of finite fields with one step function per action, naming the entity it tracks, the\n   312\t  phases it starts and ends in, its timers, its setup parameters and the observation confirming\n   313\t  each Fact; `Umpire.Command.MachineDeclaration` is its record.\n   314\t  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* A machine may instead be derived from\n   315\t  another: `from:` names the source, `restrict:` keeps the listed actions' rows and drops the rest,\n   316\t  and `extend:` adds the author's results to an action's rows, in the step order\n   317\t  (`Umpire.Command.Derived`). A derived machine keeps its source's entity, state type, starts, ends\n   318\t  and setup, and the evidence of every fact its rows still record; it reaches its rows through the\n   319\t  source's own step functions, owns its Action catalog and Definition IDs, and inherits neither\n   320\t  `refines:` nor the source's abstract state field.\n   321\t- **Entity (`Umpire.Command.Entity`).** Something with identity that a machine keeps state for,\n   322\t  declared by the `entity` command with the entities it refers to and the key recorded data names\n   323\t  an instance by. A Scenario runs over a number of `instances:` of it. State belongs to machines,\n   324\t  not entities: two machines may track one entity differently.\n   325\t- **Party.** Who performs an action: a name a Model declares by using it on an `action`. `system`\n   326\t  is reserved for the implementation under test, performs no declared action, and owns the\n   327\t  timers. A set binds every other party; a fault is an ordinary action of the party that causes\n   328\t  it.\n   329\t  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The command that builds one Model from\n   330\t  several machines is `compose`, not `system`: `system` is this reserved party, and a composition is\n   331\t  no party. It declares no action; each of its actions is one or more of its members' actions,\n   332\t  performed by their parties.\n   333\t- **Refinement (`Umpire.Command.Refinement`).** A machine that `refines:` another through a state\n   334\t  `map:`: a stuttering forward simulation the `machine` command decides over the two tables, so\n   335\t  every Property declared on the abstract machine is read on the refining machine's paths. It is\n   336\t  not an Implementation Link, which SEM-08 reserves for the connection from `Temporal.Feature` to\n   337\t  `Temporal.System`; both machines of a refinement are product behavior.\n   338\t  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* A composition does not refine and\n   339\t  carries no `refines:` or abstract field, though a member may itself be a refining machine; a\n   340\t  derived machine does not inherit its source's refinement.\n   341\t- **Composition (`Umpire.Command.Compose`).** *(drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* One\n   342\t  Model built by the `compose` command from machines of different entities, for a claim no one of\n   343\t  them can state. The author writes the state as a `structure` with one field per member, names each\n   344\t  member machine, and pairs member actions into one step with `sync:` lines; an action no `sync:`\n   345\t  line names steps its member alone. The command generates tagged-union Action, Outcome and Fact\n   346\t  types, keys a composed state by its members' state keys `_`-joined in field order, a member's own\n   347\t  action as `<field>_<key>` and a synchronized action by its `sync:` name, and hangs its Definition\n   348\t  IDs off the owner `compose-<name>`. It elaborates to `Umpire.Command.DeclaredModel` as `machine`\n   349\t  does; no set names one, so a composition answers `verify` Queries only.\n   350\t- **Table (`Umpire.FiniteTable`).** The finite row form of a Machine. `Umpire.CheckedTable` is an\n   351\t  admitted one and `Umpire.TableModelSpec` is its author record.\n   352\t  *Amendment (drafted by fn-92; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* A composition's table holds only the\n   353\t  rows reachable from its starts, enumerated at elaboration and emitted as a literal; the kernel\n   354\t  decides per composition that the literal agrees with the composition of the members' tables\n   355\t  (`Umpire.Command.ComposeProofs`).\n   356\t- **Action.** Something an author asks the Model to do, such as closing a Workflow. Requesting an\n   357\t  Action neither chooses its Model Outcome nor proves that the Action occurred at runtime. The\n   358\t  `action` command declares one with its party, the entity it creates or acts on, typed inputs\n   359\t  over `enum` domains and an optional protobuf schema (`Umpire.Command.Action`). Each member of an\n   360\t  input domain is a class, and a constructor with finite fields is one class per assignment of\n   361\t  them; a Scenario selects classes, and a machine's step function is written over them.\n   362\t- **Model Outcome.** The result the Model produces for an Action. It is an expected model result,\n   363\t  not a runtime result.\n   364\t- **Step (`Umpire.Step`).** One model step and what it produced: an `outcome`, a `state`, and a list\n   365\t  of `facts`.\n   366\t- **Fact.** A claim the Model makes at a Step, such as “the Nexus operation received a\n   367\t  cancellation.” Logs, spans, RPCs, and records are Evidence used to decide whether the claim held\n   368\t  during a Run. Facts are the `facts` of a `Umpire.Step`; the Fact domain is a Model's own type.\n   369\t- **Trace (`Umpire.Scenario.Trace`).** A starting state and a sequence of Steps. It contains no\n   370\t  runtime Evidence. One position inside a Trace is a `Umpire.ModelCoordinate`.\n   445\t  root a project hangs its families off, which namespace prefix is scaffolding, and what a Case ID\n   446\t  is rooted at are the project's declared conventions, not the surface's.\n   447\t  *Amendment (drafted by fn-85; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The surface also carries `set`, which\n   448\t  groups Queries by purpose and binds parties, and `register_switch`, by which a realization\n   449\t  registers the switches a set's `repeat:` names. Producing Cases is not part of the surface: a\n   450\t  platform declares its own Case-producing block beside its realizations (`Temporal.Case.Syntax`\n   451\t  declares `case … realizes <set> as <realization>`), which names a set and a realization and\n   452\t  defines no behavior, so `Umpire.Command` names no feature and the platform's block is where a\n   453\t  feature is named.\n   454\t  *Amendment (drafted by fn-86; awaiting GOV-02 approval.)* The command surface is the only\n   455\t  authoring path for a feature Model. The adapter derives membership relations, completeness support, and\n   475\t  exact finite Search. The\n   493\t  macro MUST derive them from declarations the author wrote and MUST NOT admit a spelling the author\n   494\t  did not declare.
